# M1c Browse: Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Fill the M1b shell with Dispatched's browse features: every peek view of an entity in a streamed, virtualised, sortable grid with filters and a detail pane, export to JSON or NDJSON as a job, entity Overview with live counts, Sessions and Rules (read-only), the Dashboard, tree counts and highlight rules, copy URL / DLQ URL / entity connection string, and the M1b carry-overs (draft connection tests, "Open sign-in page", concurrent Entra sign-ins in Settings). Everything is read-only except writing an export file.

**Architecture:**
- **Core first.** Every behaviour lives in `dispatched-core`: filter specs, message summaries and capped details, session-scoped and tail ("from the end") peeks, single-message and selected-sequence reads, streamed export files, entity details, rules, sessions. The CLI moves onto the same functions (and gains `peek --from-end/--session`, `show`, `session ls`, `rule ls`); the desktop's Tauri `ops::*` functions only validate, call core and record activity.
- **Peeks stream in pages.** The UI never asks for 10,000 messages in one call: `peek_page` returns at most 500 *summaries* (body preview, not the body), the engine's `usePeekStream` keeps requesting pages until the Top N, the scan budget or the end is reached, and the grid renders rows as they arrive. Full bodies come one message at a time (`message_detail`, capped at 4 MiB). Export runs as a cancellable background job that writes page by page to a temp file and puts it in place atomically.
- **Frontend layers stay as in M1b:** `ui/` gains DataGrid sorting/columns/row numbers, a body viewer and a toggle group; `engine/` gains the typed commands, hooks and mock fixtures; `shell/` gains the message views, detail pane, export sheet, Overview, Sessions, Rules, Dashboard and explorer options.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2024 (MSRV 1.88) with `fe2o3-amqp` 0.18, Tauri 2.11 (+ `tauri-plugin-dialog` and `tauri-plugin-clipboard-manager`, Rust side only), React 19 + TypeScript + Vite 7, Tailwind 4, TanStack Table 8 / Virtual 3 / Query 5, Vitest 3, Playwright 1.64. No new npm runtime dependencies.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-dispatched-v1-design.md` (binding: §4.2, §4.4, §4.6 read-only part, §5, §6, §8, §9, §11 M1 row). M1b handover: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-m1b-design-system-shell-design.md` ("Left for M1c", "Deviations decided during build"). Design rules: `app/DESIGN.md`, `app/PRODUCT.md`, direction contract `cd app && ~/.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable surface-brief read app/src/shell`. Format precedent: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-m1b-design-system-shell.md`.

## Global Constraints

- **Repo and branch:** `/Users/hamzaabdagic/Development/Horizon-Tech-doo/dispatched`. Create branch `m1c` from the tip of `m1b` when execution starts (`git switch -c m1c m1b`); never commit to `main` or `m1b`. The kit repo is not changed.
- **The code moved on after this plan was written:** `m1b` was still receiving fixes. Before each task, read the files it modifies as they are now. Where a snippet's surrounding code differs (a renamed helper, a new field such as `Profile.bound_sign_in`), keep the plan's behaviour, names and tests and adapt the snippet to the current code; report each such adaptation in the task report.
- **Disk hygiene (hard rule, from the user's CLAUDE.md):** build only in the repo's own `target/` and `app/node_modules/`. Never set `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, never copy the repo or a crate to build or test it. Before any heavy build (`cargo build`/`cargo test`/`cargo clippy`, `npm run e2e`, `npm run shots`, `npm run tauri build`, `docker compose up`) run `df -h /System/Volumes/Data` and **stop and tell the user if under 30 GB free**. Delete what a session created before reporting done: `app/dist-mock`, `app/playwright-report`, `app/test-results`, release bundles under `target/release/bundle/`, emulator images pulled by the session (`docker image rm`, never `docker system prune`). Every subagent prompt that may build carries this rule.
- **Read-only in M1c:** nothing receives, completes, abandons, defers, dead-letters, deletes, purges, sends, schedules, or changes entities or session state. Peeks use `com.microsoft:peek-message` only (never a receiver link), so they never lock or settle a message and never raise its delivery count. Per-session peeks never accept (lock) a session. The only write is the export file on the local disk.
- **Secrets:** connection strings, keys and tokens never appear in command responses, events, the activity log, settings, the SQLite DB, console logs, test snapshots or export files. The entity connection string is written to the clipboard by Rust and never crosses IPC. A draft connection string crosses IPC once (sheet to `profile_test_draft`/`profile_save`) and is never echoed or logged.
- **No network in tests.** Unit, component and Playwright tests use fakes, ATOM fixtures and the mock transport. The only exception is the emulator gate (`DISPATCHED_EMULATOR=1`, Docker), which CI runs in its `emulator` job.
- **Boundaries (ESLint enforces):** `app/src/ui/**` imports only itself and npm packages; `app/src/engine/**` imports only itself and npm packages; only `app/src/engine/ipc.ts` and `app/src/engine/updater.ts` import `@tauri-apps/*`; `app/src/shell/**` imports `ui`, `engine` and itself; `app/src/dev/**` may import everything. No new `@tauri-apps/plugin-*` npm packages: the dialog and clipboard plugins are used from Rust only.
- **Design rules (from `app/DESIGN.md`):** colour roles only, never hex or palette utilities. Amber (`accent`) only on dead-letter counts above zero, PROD and errors, always with an icon or text beside it (never on buttons, tabs, selection, links, rows). Selection is `selection`; focus is a 1px `fg` outline (inset in grids and trees). Success has no colour. Highlight rules use the 8px label swatch only, never text, row or border colour. No syntax colouring in the body viewer. No transitions or animation except sheets, the drawer and popovers (150ms ease-out, none under reduced motion); no shimmer. Sheets are 420px. Type sizes 11/12/13/14px, weights 400/600 only; JetBrains Mono only for machine text (IDs, sequence numbers, timestamps, bodies, filter expressions, sizes in grids); tabular numerals on every number. No modal dialogs for forms.
- **Copy:** sentence case, plain words, no em dashes, no exclamation marks, no emoji. Errors say what happened and what to do; raw engine text goes on the mono `detail` line of `Problem`. Counts: `?` unknown, `~N` approximate, `~N+` capped. Buttons are verbs naming their object ("Peek more", "Export 37 messages", "Copy body").
- **Performance:** the message grid stays smooth (no dropped frames while scrolling or holding ↓) at 10,000 rows and renders fewer than 120 row elements at any time; peek pages are requested one at a time while the grid stays interactive; one call returns at most 500 summaries (tail: 5,000); one page scans at most 5,000 messages; one load scans at most 100,000 before asking the user to continue; message bodies above 4 MiB are truncated in the detail pane.
- **IPC shapes:** core types cross IPC with their serde names (snake_case fields, kebab-case enum tags as declared below). Top-level Tauri command arguments are camelCase in TypeScript (Tauri maps `profile_id` to `profileId`). Every new command that reads the namespace takes `profileId` and refuses (`not_connected`) when that profile is no longer the connected one, like `list_entities`.
- **Errors:** cross the boundary as `CommandError { kind, message }` with the kinds in `dispatched_core::diagnostics::ERROR_KINDS`. A cancelled job reports kind `cancelled` and is logged as its message without an error.
- **Toolchain:** Rust 2024, MSRV 1.88, resolver 3. Node 22. Run `cargo fmt --all` before every Rust commit (CI runs `cargo fmt --check`). The default toolchain here is newer than 1.88 and CI builds on stable with `--locked`, so a plain build never shows that a new crate needs a newer Rust.
- **New crate pins are verified on the MSRV:** after every `cargo add` (Tasks 9 and 10), run `df -h /System/Volumes/Data` and then `cargo +1.88 check -p dispatched-app --locked` (the 1.88 toolchain is installed). Step a crate down with `cargo update -p <crate> --precise <older>` until that passes, pin the passing version as `~major.minor`, and commit `Cargo.lock`. No npm plugin package and no capability permission is added for the Rust-only plugins: `app/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` stays as it is.
- **The activity log is for what the user did:** peek pages (up to 20 per load), Overview's 10 s polling and the Dashboard's auto-refresh are recorded only when they fail (`events::tracked_failures`); user-started actions (export, copy, test, sign-out, listing sessions or rules) keep `tracked`. The mock transport logs the same way.
- **No new Keychain prompts:** any task that touches the Keychain (Task 10: refresh-token checks in the sign-in list, reading the stored connection string to copy it, sign-out) uses only the kinds of access M1b already makes and adds no Keychain item. Dev builds run via `npm run tauri:dev` (M1b's signed dev build, so Keychain access survives rebuilds); check there that the new paths show no prompt.
- **impeccable and taste:** every task that changes what a user sees (Tasks 12–22) loads the `impeccable` and `design-taste-frontend` skills and ends with the Finish review procedure below. Implementers never spawn subagents.
- **Finish review procedure (Tasks 12–22).** (1) Add the task's screens to `app/e2e/shots.spec.ts` (each entry is captured in dark and light). (2) Run `cd app && df -h /System/Volumes/Data && npm run shots -- -g "<entry names>"`; screenshots land in `app/test-results/shots/`. (3) Report the screenshot paths in the task report. The controller then dispatches the `impeccable-finish-reviewer` agent with the direction contract, `docs/design/m1b-mockup-a-dark.png` and `-light.png`, the shots, the changed components and this section's design rules; material findings are fixed in the task's fix loop before the task counts as done.
- **Commits:** one or more per task, message style as in the repo (`Area: what changed`, e.g. `Core: tail peeks and session-scoped scans`). **No attribution lines** (no "Co-Authored-By", no "Generated with"). Pushing, opening the PR, tagging and releasing need the user's explicit yes, every time. Signing in to Azure or connecting to a real namespace (Task 24) needs the user's yes and stays read-only.

**Decisions taken while planning (spec ambiguities resolved; Task 25 records them in the spec):**
1. **Per-session peek** is a broker-side `peek-message` with `session-id` (no session lock). Sources that cannot do it (`Unsupported`, which also covers every refusal of a session-scoped peek except 401/403/404/429/503, e.g. 400, 405, 410, 423, 500, 501) fall back to peeking the whole entity and keeping that session's messages client-side, so results are always correct, only slower.
2. **Session listing** merges the broker's `com.microsoft:get-message-sessions` (with `last-updated-time` = Unix epoch, which also returns empty sessions that have state, where the broker allows it) with a peek tally of the main queue (message counts and first/last enqueued per session, capped at 10,000 messages). Either source may fail alone; both failing is an error. Session *state* needs a session lock, so viewing it moves to M2 with editing.
3. **"From the end"** finds the highest sequence number with one-message peeks (gallop, then bisect; at most 200 probes), then scans back in windows that grow ×4 until it has Top N matches, reaches sequence 0, or has examined 100,000 messages. It returns the last N matches oldest first and has no "Peek more" (the end has no continuation). Top N from the end is capped at 5,000. On partitioned entities (partition bits in the sequence number) it is correct but may scan more.
4. **Grid rows are summaries.** Bodies are previews (160 characters) in the grid; the detail pane fetches the full message by sequence number and shows "no longer in this view" when it has gone. Bodies over 4 MiB are truncated for display; copy-body and copy-as-JSON are disabled for truncated bodies (export gets the whole body).
5. **Filters** are a property expression (the M1a SQL-like language), one body condition (contains, regex or JSONPath with an optional expected value) and an optional session id, all ANDed, run in the Rust scan (not in the webview).
6. **Node colouring** (spec §4.2) uses the five label swatches as an 8px marker on the tree row, from per-connection highlight rules (name regex, or a count at or above a threshold). Amber stays reserved for dead letters, PROD and errors.
7. **Which counts the tree shows**, highlight rules, the tree filter and the sub-tab entities open on are saved per connection (spec §4.1 "per-profile tree filters, default entity view"), in the connection's shell state.
8. **Dashboard rows with DLQ > 0** are marked by the amber dead-letter number (the number is the second carrier); there is no row tint. Unknown counts are blank cells in "Copy as TSV".
9. **Entity URLs** are `sb://{host}/{entity path}` and `…/$DeadLetterQueue`; the entity connection string is the profile's connection string with `EntityPath={entity}`, offered for connection-string profiles only, refused when the stored string is scoped to another entity.
10. **Export path** comes from the native save dialog (opened by Rust), which handles "replace?"; exports never overwrite without it. "Export all" uses the view's current filter and session; "Export selected" reads the selected sequence numbers again and reports any that have gone.
11. **Concurrent Entra sign-ins** (left for M1c) were mostly built late in M1b (tenant sign-ins kept beside the home one, R35). M1c adds the rest: a Settings list of every stored sign-in (whose it is, which profiles connect with it) with sign-out. "Sign in again" for a profile already signs in to that profile's tenant through M1b's "Sign in to {tenant}" (`TenantSignInAction`), so `SignInPanel` is not changed.
12. **Overview "live counts"** refresh every 10 s while the Overview is visible, plus F5.
13. **No "transfer" count in the tree's count choice or highlight rules.** Spec §4.2 lists transfer among the colouring and count thresholds; `CountKind` is `active | dead-letter | scheduled | transfer-dlq`. Transfer messages are in flight to another entity and are not browsable in M1c (there is no transfer view), so a transfer count is shown on Overview and the Dashboard but is not offered in the tree. Recorded as a deviation from §4.2.
14. **Rules are a sub-tab, not tree nodes.** Spec §4.2 has Rules nodes under subscriptions in the lazy tree; M1c shows a subscription's rules in its Rules sub-tab (Task 19) and the tree keeps entities only. Recorded as a deviation from §4.2.

## Review Focus

1. **Session-enabled entities.** The broker may refuse a whole-entity peek on an entity that requires sessions, and the emulator may not support session-scoped peeks or session listing. Expected: a session-scoped peek falls back to client-side filtering and still returns that session's messages; a refused whole-entity peek shows a Problem that names the fix ("open Sessions and peek one session"); listing sessions works when either source works. Tests: Task 2 `session_scan_falls_back_when_the_source_cannot_peek_by_session`, Task 5 `merge_keeps_broker_sessions_without_messages` and `list_fails_only_when_both_sources_fail`, Task 14 `describePeekError names the Sessions tab for session-required errors`.
2. **Huge, binary and malformed bodies:** a 100 MB Premium body, non-UTF-8 bytes, AMQP value and sequence bodies, empty bodies, `application/json` that is not JSON, XML with CDATA. Expected: the detail truncates at 4 MiB on a character boundary and says so, hex works for any bytes, formatting falls back to raw text, nothing throws. Tests: Task 1 `detail_truncates_large_bodies_on_char_boundaries`, `summary_previews_text_and_flags_binary`, Task 13 `prettyJson returns null for invalid JSON`, `prettyXml keeps CDATA and comments and the declaration`, `bytes hexDump shows non-printables as dots`, Task 16 `binary body offers hex and no text copy`.
3. **Messages and entities that change underneath the view:** a message received or expired between the grid and the detail or export, an entity deleted while its tab is open, a connection switched while pages are still loading. Expected: "This message is no longer in the dead-letter queue", export reports the missing count, stale pages for another profile are refused (`not_connected`) and dropped, nothing crashes. Tests: Task 2 `peek_one_returns_the_message_or_none_when_the_sequence_is_gone`, `collect_sequences_returns_found_and_reports_missing`, Task 7 `peek_page_for_another_profile_is_not_connected_and_not_logged`, Task 9 `selected_export_summary_counts_missing_messages`, Task 11 `peek stream ignores pages after the key changes`, Task 16 `gone message shows the gone state`.
4. **Runaway scans:** a filter that matches nothing on an entity with a million messages, Top N 50,000, from-end on sparse or partitioned sequence numbers. Expected: each load stops after 100,000 examined messages with "Scanned 100,000 messages, none matched. Peek more to keep scanning.", a Stop button works mid-load, the UI stays responsive, from-end returns within its budget. Tests: Task 2 `tail_is_bounded_by_the_scan_budget`, `last_sequence_finds_the_end_of_sparse_sequences`, `last_sequence_terminates_on_a_source_that_never_ends`, Task 11 `stream stops at the scan budget and reports it`, `stop keeps the rows already loaded`.
5. **Secrets through the new paths:** copying an entity connection string, testing an unsaved connection, export activity and files. Expected: no connection string or key in any command response, activity entry, job event or export file. Tests: Task 10 `copy_connection_string_never_returns_or_logs_the_secret`, `test_draft_activity_never_contains_the_secret`, Task 9 `export_events_name_the_file_not_the_messages`, `overwrite_is_allowed_only_for_the_path_the_dialog_returned` (the webview cannot make Rust replace an arbitrary file), Task 23 Playwright `overview counts and copying a connection string shows no secret`.

### Task 1: Core: filter specs, the session condition, message summaries and details

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dispatched-core/src/filter/spec.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/summary.rs`
- Modify: `crates/dispatched-core/src/filter/mod.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/mod.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/lib.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/scan.rs` (one test literal), `crates/dispatched-core/tests/emulator_m1.rs` (one literal), `crates/dispatched-cli/src/commands/browse.rs` (`build_filter`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: M1a `PropertyFilter::parse`, `BodyFilter::{contains, regex, json}`, `PeekedMessage`, `MessageBody`, `PropValue`.
- Produces:
  - `MessageFilter { property, body, session_id: Option<String> }` (new field; `matches` also requires `msg.session_id == session_id` when set)
  - `filter::FilterSpec { property, body_contains, body_regex, body_json_path, body_json_equals, session_id: Option<String> }` (`Serialize + Deserialize + Default`, `#[serde(default)]`), `FilterSpec::compile(&self) -> Result<MessageFilter, Vec<FilterIssue>>`, `FilterSpec::build(&self) -> Result<MessageFilter>` (first issue as `Error::Invalid`), `FilterSpec::is_empty`
  - `filter::FilterIssue { field: FilterField, message: String }`, `FilterField` = `property | body_regex | body_json_path | body_json_equals` (snake_case)
  - `messaging::summary::{MessageSummary, MessageDetail, DETAIL_BODY_CAP, PREVIEW_CHARS}`; `MessageSummary::from(&PeekedMessage)`; `MessageDetail::new(PeekedMessage, cap: usize)`
  - re-exported from the crate root: `FilterSpec, FilterIssue, FilterField, MessageSummary, MessageDetail`

```rust
    #[test]

        assert!(!f.is_empty());
```

and change the existing literal in `property_and_body_are_anded` to end with `..Default::default()` (also in `messaging/scan.rs` `filters_apply_during_the_scan` and in `tests/emulator_m1.rs` `ours`).

Create `crates/dispatched-core/src/filter/spec.rs` with only its tests first:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn blank_fields_mean_no_condition() {
        let s = FilterSpec { property: Some("  ".into()), body_regex: Some(String::new()), ..Default::default() };
        assert!(s.is_empty());
        assert!(s.build().unwrap().is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_field_compiles() {
        let s = FilterSpec {
            property: Some("sys.DeliveryCount > 1".into()),
            body_contains: Some("order".into()),
            body_regex: Some("^\\{".into()),
            body_json_path: Some("$.status".into()),
            body_json_equals: Some("\"shipped\"".into()),
            session_id: Some("RMA-1".into()),
        };
        let f = s.build().unwrap();
        assert!(f.property.is_some());
        assert_eq!(f.body.len(), 3);
        assert_eq!(f.session_id.as_deref(), Some("RMA-1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn each_bad_field_is_reported_once_with_its_field() {
        let s = FilterSpec {
            property: Some("a =".into()),
            body_regex: Some("(".into()),
            body_json_equals: Some("1".into()),
            ..Default::default()
        };
        let issues = s.compile().unwrap_err();
        let fields: Vec<FilterField> = issues.iter().map(|i| i.field).collect();
        assert_eq!(fields, [FilterField::Property, FilterField::BodyRegex, FilterField::BodyJsonEquals]);
        assert!(issues[0].message.contains("column"), "{}", issues[0].message);
        assert!(s.build().unwrap_err().to_string().contains("column"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bad_json_path_is_its_own_field() {
        let s = FilterSpec { body_json_path: Some("$[".into()), ..Default::default() };
        assert_eq!(s.compile().unwrap_err()[0].field, FilterField::BodyJsonPath);
    }

    #[test]
    fn contains_keeps_its_spaces_and_session_is_not_trimmed() {
        let s = FilterSpec { body_contains: Some(" id".into()), session_id: Some(" a".into()), ..Default::default() };
        let f = s.build().unwrap();
        let mut m = crate::messaging::test_message(1);
        m.body = crate::MessageBody::Text("order id".into());
        m.session_id = Some(" a".into());
        assert!(f.matches(&m));
        m.body = crate::MessageBody::Text("orderid".into());
        assert!(!f.matches(&m));
    }

    #[test]
    fn deserializes_from_partial_json() {
        let s: FilterSpec = serde_json::from_str(r#"{"property":"x = 1"}"#).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(s.property.as_deref(), Some("x = 1"));
        assert!(s.session_id.is_none());
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::messaging::{MessageBody, test_message};

    #[test]
    fn summary_previews_text_and_flags_binary() {
        let mut m = test_message(7);
        m.body = MessageBody::Binary(format!("{{\"id\":1,\n  \"x\":\"{}\"}}", "y".repeat(500)).into_bytes());
        let s = MessageSummary::from(&m);
        assert_eq!(s.sequence_number, 7);
        assert_eq!(s.body_kind, "binary");
        assert_eq!(s.body_size, m.body.len());
        let p = s.body_preview.unwrap();
        assert!(p.starts_with("{\"id\":1, \"x\""), "whitespace collapsed: {p}");
        assert_eq!(p.chars().count(), PREVIEW_CHARS);
        m.body = MessageBody::Binary(vec![0xff, 0xfe, 0x00]);
        let s = MessageSummary::from(&m);
        assert_eq!(s.body_preview, None, "non-UTF-8 has no text preview");
        m.body = MessageBody::Empty;
        assert_eq!(MessageSummary::from(&m).body_kind, "empty");
    }

    #[test]
    fn summary_never_carries_the_body() {
        let mut m = test_message(1);
        m.body = MessageBody::Text("z".repeat(100_000));
        let json = serde_json::to_string(&MessageSummary::from(&m)).unwrap();
        assert!(json.len() < 2_000, "{} bytes", json.len());
        assert!(!json.contains("\"body\""));
    }

    #[test]
    fn detail_truncates_large_bodies_on_char_boundaries() {
        let mut m = test_message(1);
        m.body = MessageBody::Text("é".repeat(10)); // 20 bytes
        let d = MessageDetail::new(m.clone(), 5);
        assert!(d.body_truncated);
        assert_eq!(d.body_size, 20);
        assert_eq!(d.message.body, MessageBody::Text("éé".into()), "cut before the split character");
        m.body = MessageBody::Binary(vec![1; 10]);
        let d = MessageDetail::new(m.clone(), 4);
        assert_eq!(d.message.body, MessageBody::Binary(vec![1; 4]));
        m.body = MessageBody::Value(serde_json::json!({"k": "v".repeat(20)}));
        let d = MessageDetail::new(m.clone(), 8);
        assert!(matches!(d.message.body, MessageBody::Text(ref t) if t.len() <= 8));
        let small = MessageDetail::new(m.clone(), DETAIL_BODY_CAP);
        assert!(!small.body_truncated);
        assert_eq!(small.message.body, m.body);
    }

    #[test]
    fn detail_serializes_flat() {
        let d = MessageDetail::new(test_message(3), DETAIL_BODY_CAP);
        let v = serde_json::to_value(&d).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["sequence_number"], 3);
        assert_eq!(v["body_truncated"], false);
        assert_eq!(v["body"]["kind"], "text");
    }
}
```

`crates/dispatched-core/src/filter/mod.rs`: add the module and field.

```rust
mod spec;
pub use spec::{FilterField, FilterIssue, FilterSpec};

/// Property, body and session conditions combined with AND.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct MessageFilter {
    /// Optional property expression.
    pub property: Option<PropertyFilter>,
    /// Body conditions (all must match).
    pub body: Vec<BodyFilter>,
    /// Only messages of this session (exact comparison, as Service Bus compares session ids).
    pub session_id: Option<String>,
}

impl MessageFilter {
    /// No conditions.
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.property.is_none() && self.body.is_empty() && self.session_id.is_none()
    }

    /// Whether `msg` satisfies every condition.
    pub fn matches(&self, msg: &PeekedMessage) -> bool {
        self.session_id.as_deref().is_none_or(|s| msg.session_id.as_deref() == Some(s))
            && self.property.as_ref().is_none_or(|p| p.matches(msg))
            && self.body.iter().all(|b| b.matches(&msg.body))
    }
}
```

`crates/dispatched-core/src/filter/spec.rs` (above the tests):

```rust
//! A filter as a surface submits it (plain strings), checked and compiled here so every
//! surface reports the same problems.

use super::{BodyFilter, MessageFilter, PropertyFilter};
use crate::{Error, Result};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

/// Filter fields as typed by a user. Blank strings mean "no condition".
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(default)]
pub struct FilterSpec {
    /// Property expression, e.g. `sys.DeliveryCount > 3 AND region = 'eu'`.
    pub property: Option<String>,
    /// Case-insensitive body substring (spaces are kept).
    pub body_contains: Option<String>,
    /// Body regular expression.
    pub body_regex: Option<String>,
    /// JSONPath into a JSON body.
    pub body_json_path: Option<String>,
    /// With `body_json_path`: the value a node must equal (JSON, or a bare string).
    pub body_json_equals: Option<String>,
    /// Only this session's messages (exact).
    pub session_id: Option<String>,
}

/// The field a problem belongs to.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum FilterField {
    /// `property`
    Property,
    /// `body_regex`
    BodyRegex,
    /// `body_json_path`
    BodyJsonPath,
    /// `body_json_equals`
    BodyJsonEquals,
}

/// One problem with a [`FilterSpec`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct FilterIssue {
    /// Which field.
    pub field: FilterField,
    /// What is wrong, in the words the parsers use (property errors name the column).
    pub message: String,
}

fn trimmed(s: &Option<String>) -> Option<&str> {
    s.as_deref().map(str::trim).filter(|t| !t.is_empty())
}

fn raw(s: &Option<String>) -> Option<&str> {
    s.as_deref().filter(|t| !t.is_empty())
}

impl FilterSpec {
    /// No field is set.
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        trimmed(&self.property).is_none()
            && raw(&self.body_contains).is_none()
            && trimmed(&self.body_regex).is_none()
            && trimmed(&self.body_json_path).is_none()
            && trimmed(&self.body_json_equals).is_none()
            && raw(&self.session_id).is_none()
    }

    /// Compile every field, or return every problem (at most one per field).
    pub fn compile(&self) -> std::result::Result<MessageFilter, Vec<FilterIssue>> {
        let mut issues = Vec::new();
        let mut issue = |field, e: Error| issues.push(FilterIssue { field, message: e.to_string() });
        let mut f = MessageFilter::default();
        if let Some(p) = trimmed(&self.property) {
            match PropertyFilter::parse(p) {
                Ok(pf) => f.property = Some(pf),
                Err(e) => issue(FilterField::Property, e),
            }
        }
        if let Some(t) = raw(&self.body_contains) {
            f.body.push(BodyFilter::contains(t));
        }
        if let Some(r) = trimmed(&self.body_regex) {
            match BodyFilter::regex(r) {
                Ok(b) => f.body.push(b),
                Err(e) => issue(FilterField::BodyRegex, e),
            }
        }
        match (trimmed(&self.body_json_path), trimmed(&self.body_json_equals)) {
            (Some(path), eq) => match BodyFilter::json(path, eq) {
                Ok(b) => f.body.push(b),
                Err(e) => issue(FilterField::BodyJsonPath, e),
            },
            (None, Some(_)) => issue(
                FilterField::BodyJsonEquals,
                Error::Invalid("add a JSONPath to compare this value with".into()),
            ),
            (None, None) => {}
        }
        f.session_id = raw(&self.session_id).map(str::to_string);
        if issues.is_empty() { Ok(f) } else { Err(issues) }
    }

    /// Compile, failing with the first problem.
    pub fn build(&self) -> Result<MessageFilter> {
        self.compile().map_err(|issues| {
            Error::Invalid(issues.into_iter().next().map(|i| i.message).unwrap_or_default())
        })
    }
}
```

`crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/summary.rs` (above the tests):

```rust
//! What surfaces show in lists (summaries, no body) and in a detail view (the body, capped).

use super::message::{MessageBody, MessageState, PeekedMessage, PropValue};
use serde::Serialize;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use time::OffsetDateTime;

/// Characters of body text kept in a summary's preview.
pub const PREVIEW_CHARS: usize = 160;
/// Largest body a detail view carries; larger bodies are cut and flagged.
pub const DETAIL_BODY_CAP: usize = 4 * 1024 * 1024;

/// A peeked message without its body, for grids and lists.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]
pub struct MessageSummary {
    pub sequence_number: i64,
    pub state: MessageState,
    pub message_id: Option<String>,
    pub correlation_id: Option<String>,
    pub session_id: Option<String>,
    pub subject: Option<String>,
    pub content_type: Option<String>,
    pub to: Option<String>,
    pub reply_to: Option<String>,
    #[serde(with = "time::serde::rfc3339::option")]
    pub enqueued_time: Option<OffsetDateTime>,
    #[serde(with = "time::serde::rfc3339::option")]
    pub scheduled_enqueue_time: Option<OffsetDateTime>,
    pub delivery_count: u32,
    pub time_to_live_ms: Option<u32>,
    pub dead_letter_reason: Option<String>,
    pub dead_letter_description: Option<String>,
    pub dead_letter_source: Option<String>,
    pub application_properties: BTreeMap<String, PropValue>,
    /// `binary`, `text`, `value`, `sequence` or `empty`.
    pub body_kind: &'static str,
    /// Body size in bytes (see [`MessageBody::len`]).
    pub body_size: usize,
    /// The first [`PREVIEW_CHARS`] characters of the body text with whitespace runs collapsed;
    /// `None` for empty and non-UTF-8 bodies.
    pub body_preview: Option<String>,
}

fn kind(b: &MessageBody) -> &'static str {
    match b {
        MessageBody::Binary(_) => "binary",
        MessageBody::Text(_) => "text",
        MessageBody::Value(_) => "value",
        MessageBody::Sequence(_) => "sequence",
        MessageBody::Empty => "empty",
    }
}

fn preview(b: &MessageBody) -> Option<String> {
    let text = match b {
        // Only look at the head of a large binary body.
        MessageBody::Binary(bytes) => {
            let head = &bytes[..bytes.len().min(PREVIEW_CHARS * 4)];
            match std::str::from_utf8(head) {
                Ok(s) => s.to_string(),
                // A multi-byte character cut at the end of the head is still text.
                Err(e) if e.error_len().is_none() => String::from_utf8_lossy(&head[..e.valid_up_to()]).into_owned(),
                Err(_) => return None,
            }
        }
        other => other.as_text()?,
    };
    let collapsed: String = text.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    (!collapsed.is_empty()).then(|| collapsed.chars().take(PREVIEW_CHARS).collect())
}

impl From<&PeekedMessage> for MessageSummary {
    fn from(m: &PeekedMessage) -> Self {
        Self {
            sequence_number: m.sequence_number,
            state: m.state,
            message_id: m.message_id.clone(),
            correlation_id: m.correlation_id.clone(),
            session_id: m.session_id.clone(),
            subject: m.subject.clone(),
            content_type: m.content_type.clone(),
            to: m.to.clone(),
            reply_to: m.reply_to.clone(),
            enqueued_time: m.enqueued_time,
            scheduled_enqueue_time: m.scheduled_enqueue_time,
            delivery_count: m.delivery_count,
            time_to_live_ms: m.time_to_live_ms,
            dead_letter_reason: m.dead_letter_reason.clone(),
            dead_letter_description: m.dead_letter_description.clone(),
            dead_letter_source: m.dead_letter_source.clone(),
            application_properties: m.application_properties.clone(),
            body_kind: kind(&m.body),
            body_size: m.body.len(),
            body_preview: preview(&m.body),
        }
    }
}

/// One message for a detail view, its body cut to a cap.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]
pub struct MessageDetail {
    #[serde(flatten)]
    pub message: PeekedMessage,
    /// The full body size in bytes.
    pub body_size: usize,
    /// The body was cut to the cap.
    pub body_truncated: bool,
}

impl MessageDetail {
    /// Wrap `message`, cutting a body longer than `cap` bytes (text on a character boundary;
    /// value and sequence bodies become their compact JSON text, cut).
    pub fn new(mut message: PeekedMessage, cap: usize) -> Self {
        let body_size = message.body.len();
        let body_truncated = body_size > cap;
        if body_truncated {
            let cut = |s: &str| -> String {
                let mut end = cap.min(s.len());
                while !s.is_char_boundary(end) {
                    end -= 1;
                }
                s[..end].to_string()
            };
            message.body = match std::mem::replace(&mut message.body, MessageBody::Empty) {
                MessageBody::Binary(mut b) => {
                    b.truncate(cap);
                    MessageBody::Binary(b)
                }
                MessageBody::Text(s) => MessageBody::Text(cut(&s)),
                MessageBody::Value(j) | MessageBody::Sequence(j) => MessageBody::Text(cut(&j.to_string())),
                MessageBody::Empty => MessageBody::Empty,
            };
        }
        Self { message, body_size, body_truncated }
    }
}
```

```rust
pub fn build_filter(a: &FilterArgs<'_>) -> anyhow::Result<MessageFilter> {
    if a.body_json_eq.is_some() && a.body_json.is_none() {
        anyhow::bail!("--body-json-eq needs --body-json PATH");
    }
    let spec = dispatched_core::FilterSpec {
        property: a.filter.map(str::to_string),
        body_contains: a.body_contains.map(str::to_string),
        body_regex: a.body_regex.map(str::to_string),
        body_json_path: a.body_json.map(str::to_string),
        body_json_equals: a.body_json_eq.map(str::to_string),
        session_id: a.session.map(str::to_string),
    };
    Ok(spec.build()?)
}
```

### Task 2: Core: session-scoped peeks, from the end, one message, selected sequences

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/peek.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/scan.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/messaging/mod.rs`, `crates/dispatched-core/src/explorer.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1 `MessageFilter.session_id`.
- Produces:
  - `PeekSource::peek_session_batch(&mut self, address, session_id: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>>` (default: `Err(Error::Unsupported { capability: "peek within a session" })`); `ServiceBusConnection` implements it with `peek::peek_session`
  - `scan_messages<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(src: &mut S, …)` peeks within `opts.filter.session_id` when set, falling back to whole-entity batches on `Unsupported`
  - `scan::last_sequence(src, address, session: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<i64>>`, `scan::MAX_PROBES = 200`
  - `scan::peek_tail(src, address, view, opts: &PeekOptions) -> Result<PeekPage>` (last `opts.max` matches oldest first, `exhausted: true`, `next_sequence: None`; `opts.scan_limit` is the total budget)
  - `scan::peek_one(src, address, view, sequence) -> Result<Option<PeekedMessage>>`
  - `scan::SequenceCollection { messages: Vec<PeekedMessage>, missing: Vec<i64>, scanned: u64 }`, `scan::collect_sequences(src, address, view, sequences: &[i64], budget: u64, cancel: Option<&CancellationToken>) -> Result<SequenceCollection>`
  - `Explorer::peek_tail(&self, entity, view, opts) -> Result<PeekPage>`, `Explorer::peek_message(&self, entity, view, sequence) -> Result<Option<PeekedMessage>>`, `Explorer::collect_sequences(&self, entity, view, sequences, budget, cancel) -> Result<SequenceCollection>`; `peek_tail` and `collect_sequences` take the AMQP lock per batch (private `PerBatch` source), never for a whole scan
  - private `Explorer::{connection, settle}` (Task 5's `list_sessions` uses them)
  - `parse_session_peek_response`: every non-2xx status except 401/403/404/429/503 is `Unsupported`
  - `peek_tail` keeps at most the last `opts.max` matches per window (a `VecDeque` ring)
  - `CancellationToken` is `horizon_azure_kit::safety::CancellationToken`

```rust
        // including 410 (lock lost) and 423 (locked) from a broker that wants a session lock.
        for status in [400, 405, 409, 410, 423, 500, 501] {
            let e = parse_session_peek_response(response(status, Value::Null)).unwrap_err();
            assert!(matches!(e, Error::Unsupported { .. }), "{status}: {e:?}");
        }
        assert!(matches!(parse_session_peek_response(response(401, Value::Null)).unwrap_err(), Error::Auth(_)));
        for status in [403, 404, 429, 503] {
            let e = parse_session_peek_response(response(status, Value::Null)).unwrap_err();
            assert!(!matches!(e, Error::Unsupported { .. }), "{status} keeps its own meaning: {e:?}");

        assert!(parse_session_peek_response(response(204, Value::Null)).unwrap().is_empty());
```

Then add:

```rust
    /// A source that peeks within a session (or refuses to).
    struct SessionFake {
        messages: Vec<PeekedMessage>,
        session_calls: u32,
        plain_calls: u32,
        supports_sessions: bool,
    }

    #[async_trait]
    impl PeekSource for SessionFake {
        async fn peek_batch(&mut self, _a: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
            self.plain_calls += 1;
            Ok(self.messages.iter().filter(|m| m.sequence_number >= from).take(max as usize).cloned().collect())
        }
        async fn peek_session_batch(&mut self, _a: &str, s: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
            self.session_calls += 1;
            if !self.supports_sessions {
                return Err(Error::Unsupported { capability: "peek within a session".into() });
            }
            Ok(self
                .messages
                .iter()
                .filter(|m| m.sequence_number >= from && m.session_id.as_deref() == Some(s))
                .take(max as usize)
                .cloned()
                .collect())
        }
    }

    fn sessions(n: i64) -> Vec<PeekedMessage> {
        (1..=n)
            .map(|i| {
                let mut m = test_message(i);
                m.session_id = Some(if i % 2 == 0 { "even" } else { "odd" }.into());
                m
            })
            .collect()
    }

    fn in_session(id: &str) -> PeekOptions {
        PeekOptions::new(100).filter(MessageFilter { session_id: Some(id.into()), ..Default::default() })
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn session_scan_asks_the_broker_for_that_session() {
        let mut src = SessionFake { messages: sessions(10), session_calls: 0, plain_calls: 0, supports_sessions: true };
        let page = scan_messages(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &in_session("even")).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(seq_numbers(&page), [2, 4, 6, 8, 10]);
        assert_eq!(src.plain_calls, 0);
        assert_eq!(page.scanned, 5, "only the session's messages were examined");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn session_scan_falls_back_when_the_source_cannot_peek_by_session() {
        let mut src = SessionFake { messages: sessions(10), session_calls: 0, plain_calls: 0, supports_sessions: false };
        let page = scan_messages(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &in_session("odd")).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(seq_numbers(&page), [1, 3, 5, 7, 9]);
        assert_eq!(src.session_calls, 1, "asked once, then fell back for good");
        assert!(page.exhausted);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn last_sequence_finds_the_end_of_sparse_sequences() {
        for list in [vec![1, 2, 3], vec![5], vec![1, 10, 1_000, 1_000_000], vec![3, 4, (1i64 << 48) + 7]] {
            let mut src = Fake { messages: seqs(&list), calls: 0 };
            assert_eq!(last_sequence(&mut src, "q", None).await.unwrap(), list.last().copied(), "{list:?}");
            assert!(src.calls <= 2 * 64 + 2, "{} probes for {list:?}", src.calls);
        }
        let mut empty = Fake { messages: vec![], calls: 0 };
        assert_eq!(last_sequence(&mut empty, "q", None).await.unwrap(), None);
    }

    /// Answers every peek with a message just past the request, as if messages kept arriving.
    struct Endless {
        calls: u32,
    }

    #[async_trait]
    impl PeekSource for Endless {
        async fn peek_batch(&mut self, _a: &str, from: i64, _max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
            self.calls += 1;
            Ok(vec![test_message(from.saturating_add(1))])
        }
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn last_sequence_terminates_on_a_source_that_never_ends() {
        let mut src = Endless { calls: 0 };
        let r = last_sequence(&mut src, "q", None).await;
        assert!(src.calls <= MAX_PROBES, "{} probes", src.calls);
        assert!(matches!(r, Ok(Some(_)) | Err(Error::Protocol(_))), "{r:?}");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn tail_returns_the_last_matches_oldest_first() {
        let mut src = entity(1_000, 0);
        let opts = PeekOptions::new(3).scan_limit(100_000);
        let page = peek_tail(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &opts).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(seq_numbers(&page), [998, 999, 1000]);
        assert!(page.exhausted && page.next_sequence.is_none());
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn tail_widens_until_the_filter_has_enough_matches() {
        // Only every 100th message is scheduled; ask for the last 4 scheduled ones.
        let mut src = entity(2_000, 100);
        let opts = PeekOptions::new(4).scan_limit(100_000);
        let page = peek_tail(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Scheduled, &opts).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(seq_numbers(&page), [1700, 1800, 1900, 2000]);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn tail_window_spanning_several_pages_keeps_the_last_matches() {
        // 1,500 wanted: the window is read in pages of at most 1,000 matches into a ring.
        let mut src = entity(3_000, 0);
        let page = peek_tail(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &PeekOptions::new(1_500).scan_limit(100_000)).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(seq_numbers(&page), (1_501..=3_000).collect::<Vec<i64>>());
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn tail_of_a_short_entity_is_all_of_it() {
        let mut src = entity(5, 0);
        let page = peek_tail(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &PeekOptions::new(50).scan_limit(1_000)).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(seq_numbers(&page), [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn tail_is_bounded_by_the_scan_budget() {
        // Nothing matches; the search must stop near the budget, not read 50,000 messages.
        let mut src = entity(50_000, 0);
        let opts = PeekOptions::new(10).scan_limit(2_000);
        let page = peek_tail(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Scheduled, &opts).await.unwrap();
        assert!(page.messages.is_empty());
        assert!(page.scanned <= 2_000, "scanned {}", page.scanned);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn peek_one_returns_the_message_or_none_when_the_sequence_is_gone() {
        let mut src = Fake { messages: seqs(&[1, 2, 5]), calls: 0 };
        assert_eq!(peek_one(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, 2).await.unwrap().unwrap().sequence_number, 2);
        assert!(peek_one(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, 3).await.unwrap().is_none(), "3 was received; 5 is not it");
        assert!(peek_one(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Scheduled, 1).await.unwrap().is_none(), "wrong view");
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn collect_sequences_returns_found_and_reports_missing() {
        let mut src = Fake { messages: seqs(&[1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13, 21]), calls: 0 };
        let c = collect_sequences(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &[21, 3, 4, 3, 8], 1_000, None).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(c.messages.iter().map(|m| m.sequence_number).collect::<Vec<_>>(), [3, 8, 21]);
        assert_eq!(c.missing, [4]);
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn collect_sequences_stops_at_the_budget_and_on_cancel() {
        let mut src = entity(10_000, 0);
        let c = collect_sequences(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &[1, 9_999], 500, None).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(c.messages.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(c.missing, [9_999], "not reached within the budget");
        let token = CancellationToken::new();
        token.cancel();
        let e = collect_sequences(&mut src, "q", PeekView::Active, &[1], 500, Some(&token)).await.unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(e, Error::Cancelled));
    }
```

`peek.rs`:

```rust
/// Request body for a peek within one session (no session lock is taken).
pub fn session_peek_request(from_sequence: i64, count: i32, session_id: &str) -> OrderedMap<String, Value> {
    let mut body = peek_request(from_sequence, count);
    body.insert("session-id".to_string(), Value::String(session_id.to_string()));
    body
}

/// Decode a session peek. Every refusal except 401/403 (auth), 404 (entity gone), 429
/// (throttled) and 503 (busy) means "can't peek by session here" (a broker that wants a
/// session lock may answer 410 or 423) and becomes `Unsupported`, so callers fall back to
/// peeking the whole entity instead of retrying and dropping the connection.
pub fn parse_session_peek_response(resp: MgmtResponse) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
    match resp.status {
        200..=299 | 401 | 403 | 404 | 429 | 503 => parse_peek_response(resp),
        _ => Err(Error::Unsupported { capability: "peek within a session".into() }),
    }
}

/// Peek up to `max` messages of `session_id` in `address`, starting at `from_sequence`.
pub async fn peek_session(
    conn: &mut ServiceBusConnection,
    address: &str,
    session_id: &str,
    from_sequence: i64,
    max: u32,
) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
    let count = i32::try_from(max.clamp(1, MAX_PER_REQUEST)).expect("bounded by MAX_PER_REQUEST");
    let link = conn.management(address).await?;
    let resp = link.call(OPERATION, session_peek_request(from_sequence, count, session_id)).await?;
    parse_session_peek_response(resp)
}
```

`scan.rs`: trait default, connection impl, the batch helper and the new functions:

```rust
use crate::Error;
use horizon_azure_kit::safety::CancellationToken;
use std::collections::{BTreeSet, VecDeque};

#[async_trait]
pub trait PeekSource: Send {
    /// Peek up to `max` messages from `address` starting at sequence `from`.
    async fn peek_batch(&mut self, address: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>>;

    /// Peek within one session. Sources that cannot answer `Unsupported`; callers then peek
    /// the whole entity and keep the session's messages themselves.
    async fn peek_session_batch(
        &mut self,
        _address: &str,
        _session_id: &str,
        _from: i64,
        _max: u32,
    ) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
        Err(Error::Unsupported { capability: "peek within a session".into() })
    }
}

#[async_trait]
impl PeekSource for ServiceBusConnection {
    async fn peek_batch(&mut self, address: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
        peek::peek(self, address, from, max).await
    }
    async fn peek_session_batch(&mut self, address: &str, session_id: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
        peek::peek_session(self, address, session_id, from, max).await
    }
}

/// Batches from `address`, within `session` while the source can peek by session.
struct Batches<'a, S: PeekSource + ?Sized> {
    src: &'a mut S,
    address: &'a str,
    session: Option<&'a str>,
}

impl<S: PeekSource + ?Sized> Batches<'_, S> {
    async fn next(&mut self, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
        if let Some(id) = self.session {
            match self.src.peek_session_batch(self.address, id, from, max).await {
                Err(Error::Unsupported { .. }) => self.session = None,
                other => return other,
            }
        }
        self.src.peek_batch(self.address, from, max).await
    }
}
```

`scan_messages` becomes generic (`pub async fn scan_messages<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(src: &mut S, address: &str, view: PeekView, opts: &PeekOptions)`), builds `let mut batches = Batches { src, address, session: opts.filter.session_id.as_deref() };` and calls `batches.next(next, want)` where it called `src.peek_batch(address, next, want)`. Nothing else in it changes. The session condition in `MessageFilter::matches` keeps the result correct after a fallback.

```rust
/// Most one-message peeks [`last_sequence`] makes.
pub const MAX_PROBES: u32 = 200;

async fn first_at<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(b: &mut Batches<'_, S>, from: i64, probes: &mut u32) -> Result<Option<i64>> {
    *probes += 1;
    if *probes > MAX_PROBES {
        return Err(Error::Protocol("could not find the end of the entity: sequence numbers kept moving".into()));
    }
    Ok(b.next(from, 1).await?.into_iter().map(|m| m.sequence_number).find(|&s| s >= from))
}

/// The highest sequence number in `address` (within `session` when given), or `None` when it
/// is empty. Uses one-message peeks: from the first message, the step doubles until a peek
/// finds nothing, then the gap is bisected (about 2·log2(span) peeks, at most [`MAX_PROBES`]).
/// Messages that arrive meanwhile can only raise the answer.
pub async fn last_sequence<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(src: &mut S, address: &str, session: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<i64>> {
    let mut b = Batches { src, address, session };
    let mut probes = 0;
    let Some(mut lo) = first_at(&mut b, 0, &mut probes).await? else { return Ok(None) };
    let mut step: i64 = 1;
    let mut hi = loop {
        let at = lo.saturating_add(step);
        if at == lo {
            return Ok(Some(lo));
        }
        match first_at(&mut b, at, &mut probes).await? {
            Some(s) => {
                lo = s;
                step = step.saturating_mul(2);
            }
            None => break at,
        }
    };
    // A message at `lo`, nothing at or after `hi`.
    while hi - lo > 1 {
        let mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
        match first_at(&mut b, mid, &mut probes).await? {
            Some(s) => {
                lo = s;
                if lo >= hi {
                    hi = lo.saturating_add(1);
                }
            }
            None => hi = mid,
        }
    }
    Ok(Some(lo))
}

fn tail_page(kept: VecDeque<PeekedMessage>, scanned: u32) -> PeekPage {
    PeekPage { messages: kept.into(), scanned, next_sequence: None, exhausted: true }
}

/// Most matches one page of a [`peek_tail`] window holds (besides the kept tail).
const TAIL_PAGE: u32 = 1_000;

/// One window of [`peek_tail`].
struct Window {
    /// The last `want` matches seen, oldest first.
    kept: VecDeque<PeekedMessage>,
    /// Messages examined.
    scanned: u32,
    /// The end of the entity was reached within the budget.
    complete: bool,
}

/// Scan from `start` to the end of `view` within `budget`, keeping only the last `want` matches
/// in a ring. A window over large bodies therefore holds at most `want` plus one page of
/// matches, however many messages match in it.
async fn scan_window<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(
    src: &mut S,
    address: &str,
    view: PeekView,
    filter: &MessageFilter,
    start: i64,
    budget: u32,
    want: usize,
) -> Result<Window> {
    let mut w = Window { kept: VecDeque::with_capacity(want), scanned: 0, complete: false };
    let mut opts = PeekOptions { from_sequence: start, max: TAIL_PAGE, scan_limit: budget, filter: filter.clone() };
    while w.scanned < budget {
        opts.scan_limit = budget - w.scanned;
        let page = scan_messages(src, address, view, &opts).await?;
        w.scanned = w.scanned.saturating_add(page.scanned);
        for m in page.messages {
            if w.kept.len() == want {
                w.kept.pop_front();
            }
            w.kept.push_back(m);
        }
        match page.next_sequence {
            None => {
                w.complete = true;
                break;
            }
            Some(n) if n > opts.from_sequence => opts.from_sequence = n,
            // No progress: the end was not confirmed.
            Some(_) => break,
        }
    }
    Ok(w)
}

/// The last `opts.max` messages of `view` matching `opts.filter`, oldest first ("from the end").
/// Finds the end with [`last_sequence`], then scans windows that start further back (×4 each
/// time) until it has enough matches, starts at sequence 0, or has examined `opts.scan_limit`
/// messages in total. A window that runs out of budget before the end is discarded in favour
/// of the previous complete one. Each window keeps only its last `opts.max` matches, so memory
/// stays bounded on large-body entities. The page has no continuation.
pub async fn peek_tail<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(src: &mut S, address: &str, view: PeekView, opts: &PeekOptions) -> Result<PeekPage> {
    let want = opts.max as usize;
    if want == 0 {
        return Ok(tail_page(VecDeque::new(), 0));
    }
    let Some(last) = last_sequence(src, address, opts.filter.session_id.as_deref()).await? else {
        return Ok(tail_page(VecDeque::new(), 0));
    };
    let mut window = (want as i64).max(64);
    let mut scanned: u32 = 0;
    let mut best: Option<VecDeque<PeekedMessage>> = None;
    loop {
        let start = last.saturating_sub(window - 1).max(0);
        let budget = opts.scan_limit.saturating_sub(scanned).max(1);
        let w = scan_window(src, address, view, &opts.filter, start, budget, want).await?;
        scanned = scanned.saturating_add(w.scanned);
        if !w.complete {
            // Out of budget before reaching the end: this window is incomplete.
            return Ok(tail_page(best.unwrap_or(w.kept), scanned));
        }
        if w.kept.len() >= want || start == 0 || scanned >= opts.scan_limit {
            return Ok(tail_page(w.kept, scanned));
        }
        best = Some(w.kept);
        window = window.saturating_mul(4);
    }
}

/// The message with sequence number `sequence` in `view` of `address`, if it is still there.
pub async fn peek_one<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(src: &mut S, address: &str, view: PeekView, sequence: i64) -> Result<Option<PeekedMessage>> {
    let batch = src.peek_batch(address, sequence, 1).await?;
    Ok(batch.into_iter().find(|m| m.sequence_number == sequence && view.accepts(m.state)))
}

/// Messages found by [`collect_sequences`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct SequenceCollection {
    /// Found, in sequence order.
    pub messages: Vec<PeekedMessage>,
    /// Asked for but not found (received, expired, not in this view, or beyond the budget).
    pub missing: Vec<i64>,
    /// Messages examined.
    pub scanned: u64,
}

/// The messages of `view` with the given sequence numbers, read by scanning from the smallest
/// to the largest. Examines at most `budget` messages; stops early when all are found.
pub async fn collect_sequences<S: PeekSource + ?Sized>(
    src: &mut S,
    address: &str,
    view: PeekView,
    sequences: &[i64],
    budget: u64,
    cancel: Option<&CancellationToken>,
) -> Result<SequenceCollection> {
    let mut wanted: BTreeSet<i64> = sequences.iter().copied().filter(|&s| s >= 0).collect();
    let mut out = SequenceCollection::default();
    let (Some(&first), Some(&last)) = (wanted.first(), wanted.last()) else { return Ok(out) };
    let mut next = first;
    'outer: while !wanted.is_empty() && out.scanned < budget {
        if cancel.is_some_and(CancellationToken::is_cancelled) {
            return Err(Error::Cancelled);
        }
        let batch = src.peek_batch(address, next, MAX_PER_REQUEST).await?;
        let mut advanced = false;
        for m in batch {
            if m.sequence_number < next {
                continue;
            }
            if m.sequence_number > last {
                break 'outer;
            }
            out.scanned += 1;
            next = m.sequence_number + 1;
            advanced = true;
            if wanted.remove(&m.sequence_number) && view.accepts(m.state) {
                out.messages.push(m);
            }
            if out.scanned >= budget {
                break 'outer;
            }
        }
        if !advanced {
            break;
        }
    }
    // A wanted message in the wrong state was removed from `wanted` but not collected: report it.
    let found: BTreeSet<i64> = out.messages.iter().map(|m| m.sequence_number).collect();
    out.missing = sequences
        .iter()
        .copied()
        .filter(|s| *s >= 0 && !found.contains(s))
        .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()
        .into_iter()
        .collect();
    Ok(out)
}
```

`messaging/mod.rs`: `pub use scan::{…, SequenceCollection, collect_sequences, last_sequence, peek_one, peek_tail};`.

`explorer.rs`: the same open-on-demand and drop-on-connection-failure pattern as `peek_once`, factored so each operation stays short. Long scans (`peek_tail` up to 100,000 messages plus 200 probes, `collect_sequences` with an export budget of 1,000,000) must not hold the single AMQP mutex for their whole length, or the detail pane, other tabs' pages and counts wait until they end. They run over `PerBatch`, a `PeekSource` that takes the lock for one batch at a time:

```rust
use crate::messaging::{SequenceCollection, collect_sequences, peek_one, peek_tail};
use async_trait::async_trait;
use horizon_azure_kit::safety::CancellationToken;
use tokio::sync::MutexGuard;

type Guard<'a> = MutexGuard<'a, Option<ServiceBusConnection>>;

/// The explorer's cached connection as a [`PeekSource`] that holds the connection lock for one
/// batch only, so other operations interleave between the batches of a long scan. A
/// connection-level failure drops the cached connection, as `settle` does.
struct PerBatch<'a> {
    explorer: &'a Explorer,
}

#[async_trait]
impl PeekSource for PerBatch<'_> {
    async fn peek_batch(&mut self, address: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
        let mut guard = self.explorer.connection().await?;
        let result = guard.as_mut().expect("opened").peek_batch(address, from, max).await;
        Explorer::settle(&mut guard, result).await
    }

    async fn peek_session_batch(&mut self, address: &str, session_id: &str, from: i64, max: u32) -> Result<Vec<PeekedMessage>> {
        let mut guard = self.explorer.connection().await?;
        let result = guard.as_mut().expect("opened").peek_session_batch(address, session_id, from, max).await;
        Explorer::settle(&mut guard, result).await
    }
}

impl Explorer {
    /// The cached connection, opened on demand. Hold the guard for the whole operation.
    async fn connection(&self) -> Result<Guard<'_>> {
        let mut guard = self.amqp.lock().await;
        if guard.is_none() {
            *guard = Some(ServiceBusConnection::open(&self.ns).await?);
        }
        Ok(guard)
    }

    /// Pass `result` through, dropping the cached connection after a connection-level failure.
    async fn settle<T>(guard: &mut Guard<'_>, result: Result<T>) -> Result<T> {
        if result.as_ref().is_err_and(|e| retry_after(e, 0))
            && let Some(conn) = guard.take()
        {
            let _ = conn.close().await;
        }
        result
    }

    /// The last `opts.max` matches of `view` ("from the end"); see [`peek_tail`]. Locks the
    /// connection per batch ([`PerBatch`]), not for the whole search.
    pub async fn peek_tail(&self, entity: &EntityPath, view: PeekView, opts: &PeekOptions) -> Result<PeekPage> {
        let address = entity.address(view.sub_queue());
        with_one_retry(|_| async { peek_tail(&mut PerBatch { explorer: self }, &address, view, opts).await }).await
    }

    /// One message by sequence number, if it is still in `view`.
    pub async fn peek_message(&self, entity: &EntityPath, view: PeekView, sequence: i64) -> Result<Option<PeekedMessage>> {
        let address = entity.address(view.sub_queue());
        with_one_retry(|_| async {
            let mut guard = self.connection().await?;
            let result = peek_one(guard.as_mut().expect("opened"), &address, view, sequence).await;
            Self::settle(&mut guard, result).await
        })
        .await
    }

    /// Selected messages again, by sequence number; see [`collect_sequences`]. Not retried
    /// (a retry would repeat a long scan); the caller reports the error. Locks the connection
    /// per batch ([`PerBatch`]), so an export does not block browsing.
    pub async fn collect_sequences(
        &self,
        entity: &EntityPath,
        view: PeekView,
        sequences: &[i64],
        budget: u64,
        cancel: Option<&CancellationToken>,
    ) -> Result<SequenceCollection> {
        let address = entity.address(view.sub_queue());
        collect_sequences(&mut PerBatch { explorer: self }, &address, view, sequences, budget, cancel).await
    }
}
```

Rewrite the existing `peek_once` and `count_once` on top of `connection`/`settle` (same behaviour, less code; `peek()` already locks per page). Add `use crate::PeekedMessage;` as needed. `Explorer` is `Sync` (it is shared as `Arc<Explorer>`), so `PerBatch` satisfies `PeekSource: Send`.

```rust
    #[tokio::test]
    async fn unbounded_stream_reads_to_the_end_and_cancel_stops_it() {
        let pages = vec![page(&[1, 2], 2, Some(3), false), page(&[3], 1, None, true)];
        let fake = Fake::new(pages);
        let mut n = 0;
        let s = stream_pages(None, 0, None, |f, m| fake.fetch(f, m), |msgs, _| { n += msgs.len(); Ok(()) }).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!((n, s.written, s.end), (3, 3, Continuation::End));
        let token = CancellationToken::new();
        token.cancel();
        let fake = Fake::new(vec![page(&[1], 1, None, true)]);
        let e = stream_pages(None, 0, Some(&token), |f, m| fake.fetch(f, m), |_, _| Ok(())).await.unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(e, Error::Cancelled));
    }

    #[test]
    fn streamed_json_is_byte_identical_to_the_one_shot_writer() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        for count in [0usize, 1, 2, 5] {
            let msgs: Vec<PeekedMessage> = two().into_iter().cycle().take(count).collect();
            let mut expected = Vec::new();
            write_messages(&mut expected, &msgs, ExportFormat::Json).unwrap();
            let path = dir.path().join(format!("out-{count}.json"));
            let mut f = FileExport::create(&path, ExportFormat::Json, false).unwrap();
            for chunk in msgs.chunks(2) {
                f.write(chunk).unwrap();
            }
            assert_eq!(f.finish().unwrap(), count as u64);
            assert_eq!(std::fs::read(&path).unwrap(), expected, "{count} messages");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn ndjson_file_matches_the_one_shot_writer() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("out.ndjson");
        let mut f = FileExport::create(&path, ExportFormat::Ndjson, false).unwrap();
        f.write(&two()).unwrap();
        f.finish().unwrap();
        let mut expected = Vec::new();
        write_messages(&mut expected, &two(), ExportFormat::Ndjson).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read(&path).unwrap(), expected);
    }

    fn names(dir: &std::path::Path) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut v: Vec<String> = std::fs::read_dir(dir).unwrap().map(|e| e.unwrap().file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned()).collect();
        v.sort();
        v
    }

    #[test]
    fn existing_file_is_refused_without_overwrite_and_replaced_with_it() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("x.json");
        std::fs::write(&path, "old").unwrap();
        let e = FileExport::create(&path, ExportFormat::Json, false).unwrap_err();
        assert!(e.to_string().contains("already exists"), "{e}");
        let mut f = FileExport::create(&path, ExportFormat::Json, true).unwrap();
        f.write(&two()).unwrap();
        f.finish().unwrap();
        assert!(std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap().starts_with('['));
        assert_eq!(names(dir.path()), ["x.json"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_unfinished_export_leaves_nothing_behind_and_the_original_intact() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("keep.json");
        std::fs::write(&path, "original").unwrap();
        {
            let mut f = FileExport::create(&path, ExportFormat::Json, true).unwrap();
            f.write(&two()).unwrap();
            // dropped without finish (cancelled or failed)
        }
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap(), "original");
        assert_eq!(names(dir.path()), ["keep.json"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_target_that_appears_during_the_write_is_not_overwritten() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = dir.path().join("race.json");
        let mut f = FileExport::create(&path, ExportFormat::Json, false).unwrap();
        f.write(&two()).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(&path, "someone else").unwrap();
        assert!(f.finish().unwrap_err().to_string().contains("already exists"));
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap(), "someone else");
        assert_eq!(names(dir.path()), ["race.json"]);
    }

    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn devices_are_written_directly() {
        let mut f = FileExport::create(std::path::Path::new("/dev/null"), ExportFormat::Ndjson, false).unwrap();
        f.write(&two()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(f.finish().unwrap(), 2);
    }

    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn a_fifo_is_written_directly() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let fifo = dir.path().join("pipe");
        match std::process::Command::new("mkfifo").arg(&fifo).status() {
            Ok(s) if s.success() => {}
            _ => {
                eprintln!("skipping: mkfifo is not available");
                return;
            }
        }
        assert!(is_special(&fifo));
        let reader = {
            let fifo = fifo.clone();
            std::thread::spawn(move || std::fs::read_to_string(fifo).unwrap())
        };
        // No overwrite, and no temp file or rename: the FIFO itself is written.
        let mut f = FileExport::create(&fifo, ExportFormat::Ndjson, false).unwrap();
        f.write(&two()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(f.finish().unwrap(), 2);
        let got = reader.join().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(got.lines().count(), 2, "{got}");
        assert_eq!(names(dir.path()), ["pipe"]);
        assert!(!std::fs::metadata(&fifo).unwrap().is_file(), "the FIFO must not be replaced by a regular file");
    }

    #[test]
    fn io_errors_name_the_fix_without_an_em_dash() {
        let e = FileExport::create(std::path::Path::new("/nonexistent-dir/x.json"), ExportFormat::Json, false).unwrap_err();
        let text = e.to_string();
        assert!(text.contains("check the output path"), "{text}");
        assert!(!text.contains('\u{2014}'));
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `crates/dispatched-core/src/export.rs`:

```rust
use crate::messaging::PeekPage;
use horizon_azure_kit::safety::CancellationToken;
use std::fs::File;
use std::future::Future;
use std::io::BufWriter;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};

/// The largest number of matches requested in one export page.
pub const EXPORT_PAGE: u32 = 1000;

/// How a paged read ended.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Continuation {
    /// The end of the entity was reached.
    End,
    /// Stopped early; continue from this sequence number.
    More(i64),
    /// The scan made no progress, so the end of the entity was not confirmed.
    Stalled,
    /// Everything asked for was read; no continuation is known or needed.
    Done,
}

/// How `page` ended.
pub fn continuation(page: &PeekPage) -> Continuation {
    if page.exhausted {
        Continuation::End
    } else {
        page.next_sequence.map_or(Continuation::Stalled, Continuation::More)
    }
}

/// Totals of [`stream_pages`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Streamed {
    /// Messages handed to the sink.
    pub written: u64,
    /// Messages examined.
    pub scanned: u64,
    /// How it ended.
    pub end: Continuation,
}

/// Read pages until `top` messages (all when `None`) or the end, handing each page to `sink`
/// as it arrives. Each page's `next_sequence` feeds the next request; a page that does not
/// move forward ends the read as `Stalled`. Checks `cancel` before every page.
pub async fn stream_pages<F, Fut>(
    top: Option<u64>,
    from_seq: i64,
    cancel: Option<&CancellationToken>,
    mut fetch: F,
    mut sink: impl FnMut(Vec<PeekedMessage>, u64) -> Result<()>,
) -> Result<Streamed>
where
    F: FnMut(i64, u32) -> Fut,
    Fut: Future<Output = Result<PeekPage>>,
{
    let (mut written, mut scanned, mut from) = (0u64, 0u64, from_seq);
    let end = loop {
        if cancel.is_some_and(CancellationToken::is_cancelled) {
            return Err(Error::Cancelled);
        }
        let remaining = top.map_or(u64::from(EXPORT_PAGE), |t| t.saturating_sub(written));
        let page = fetch(from, remaining.min(u64::from(EXPORT_PAGE)) as u32).await?;
        scanned += u64::from(page.scanned);
        let end = continuation(&page);
        let mut messages = page.messages;
        if let Some(t) = top {
            messages.truncate(t.saturating_sub(written) as usize);
        }
        written += messages.len() as u64;
        sink(messages, scanned)?;
        let full = top.is_some_and(|t| written >= t);
        match end {
            Continuation::More(n) if !full => {
                if n <= from {
                    break Continuation::Stalled;
                }
                from = n;
            }
            Continuation::Stalled if full => break Continuation::Done,
            other => break other,
        }
    };
    Ok(Streamed { written, scanned, end })
}

fn io(e: impl std::fmt::Display) -> Error {
    Error::Io(format!("cannot write export: {e}; check the output path, permissions and free space"))
}

fn exists(path: &Path) -> Error {
    Error::Invalid(format!("{} already exists; choose another file or allow replacing it", path.display()))
}

/// A target that is not a regular file (a device or FIFO) is written directly and is never an
/// "existing file" that needs permission to replace. The CLI's `check_output` uses this too.
pub fn is_special(path: &Path) -> bool {
    std::fs::metadata(path).is_ok_and(|m| !m.is_file() && !m.is_dir())
}

fn temp_path(path: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    static NEXT: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
    let name = path.file_name().map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().into_owned()).unwrap_or_else(|| "export".into());
    let n = NEXT.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    path.with_file_name(format!(".{name}.dispatched-tmp-{}-{n}", std::process::id()))
}

/// An export file written page by page. Regular files are written to a sibling temp file
/// that `finish` syncs and puts in place atomically: with `overwrite` it is renamed over the
/// target; without, a hard link creates the target only if it is still absent (falling back
/// to a check and a rename where hard links are unsupported). Dropping an unfinished export
/// removes the temp file; an existing target is never touched by a failed or cancelled export.
pub struct FileExport {
    target: PathBuf,
    tmp: Option<PathBuf>,
    out: Option<BufWriter<File>>,
    format: ExportFormat,
    overwrite: bool,
    written: u64,
}

impl FileExport {
    /// Start an export to `path`. Fails at once when a regular file is there and
    /// `overwrite` is off.
    pub fn create(path: &Path, format: ExportFormat, overwrite: bool) -> Result<Self> {
        if is_special(path) {
            let file = std::fs::OpenOptions::new().write(true).open(path).map_err(io)?;
            return Ok(Self { target: path.into(), tmp: None, out: Some(BufWriter::new(file)), format, overwrite, written: 0 });
        }
        if !overwrite && path.exists() {
            return Err(exists(path));
        }
        let tmp = temp_path(path);
        let file = std::fs::OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true).open(&tmp).map_err(io)?;
        Ok(Self { target: path.into(), tmp: Some(tmp), out: Some(BufWriter::new(file)), format, overwrite, written: 0 })
    }

    /// Messages written so far.
    pub fn written(&self) -> u64 {
        self.written
    }

    /// Append `messages`.
    pub fn write(&mut self, messages: &[PeekedMessage]) -> Result<()> {
        let w = self.out.as_mut().expect("open until finish");
        for m in messages {
            let item = ExportedMessage::new(m);
            match self.format {
                ExportFormat::Ndjson => {
                    serde_json::to_writer(&mut *w, &item).map_err(io)?;
                    w.write_all(b"\n").map_err(io)?;
                }
                ExportFormat::Json => {
                    w.write_all(if self.written == 0 { b"[\n" } else { b",\n" }).map_err(io)?;
                    let pretty = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&item).map_err(io)?;
                    for (i, line) in pretty.lines().enumerate() {
                        if i > 0 {
                            w.write_all(b"\n").map_err(io)?;
                        }
                        w.write_all(b"  ").map_err(io)?;
                        w.write_all(line.as_bytes()).map_err(io)?;
                    }
                }
            }
            self.written += 1;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Close the document, flush, sync and put the file in place. Returns the message count.
    pub fn finish(mut self) -> Result<u64> {
        let mut w = self.out.take().expect("open until finish");
        if self.format == ExportFormat::Json {
            w.write_all(if self.written == 0 { b"[]\n" } else { b"\n]\n" }).map_err(io)?;
        }
        w.flush().map_err(io)?;
        let file = w.into_inner().map_err(|e| io(e.into_error()))?;
        let Some(tmp) = self.tmp.clone() else { return Ok(self.written) };
        file.sync_all().map_err(io)?;
        drop(file);
        if self.overwrite {
            std::fs::rename(&tmp, &self.target).map_err(io)?;
            self.tmp = None;
            return Ok(self.written);
        }
        match std::fs::hard_link(&tmp, &self.target) {
            // Drop removes the temp name; the target keeps the data.
            Ok(()) => Ok(self.written),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists => Err(exists(&self.target)),
            Err(_) => {
                if self.target.exists() {
                    return Err(exists(&self.target));
                }
                std::fs::rename(&tmp, &self.target).map_err(io)?;
                self.tmp = None;
                Ok(self.written)
            }
        }
    }
}

impl Drop for FileExport {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if let Some(tmp) = self.tmp.take() {
            let _ = std::fs::remove_file(tmp);
        }
    }
}
```

(`write_messages` stays as it is for stdout; `ExportFormat` gains `#[derive(…, PartialEq, Eq)]` if not already present. `lib.rs`: `pub use export::{Continuation, EXPORT_PAGE, FileExport, Streamed, continuation, stream_pages, …};`.)

CLI `export` in `browse.rs`:

```rust
pub async fn export(ctx: &Ctx, sel: &Selection<'_>, args: &ViewArgs, top: u32, out: &str, format: ExportFormat, force: bool) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    check_output(out, force)?;
    let q = Query::open(ctx, sel, args)?;
    let qr = &q;
    let fetch = |from, max| {
        let opts = qr.options(max, from);
        async move { qr.explorer.peek(&qr.entity, qr.view, &opts).await }
    };
    let result = if out == "-" {
        let mut all = Vec::new();
        let s = stream_pages(Some(u64::from(top)), q.from_seq, None, fetch, |m, _| { all.extend(m); Ok(()) }).await;
        s.and_then(|s| write_messages(std::io::stdout().lock(), &all, format).map(|_| s))
    } else {
        let mut file = FileExport::create(std::path::Path::new(out), format, force)?;
        let s = stream_pages(Some(u64::from(top)), q.from_seq, None, fetch, |m, _| file.write(&m)).await;
        s.and_then(|s| file.finish().map(|_| s))
    };
    q.explorer.close().await;
    let s = result?;
    let n = s.written as usize;
    let dest = if out == "-" { "stdout".to_string() } else { sanitize(out) };
    eprintln!("Exported {n} message(s) to {dest}");
    print_notes(&scan_notes(&ScanSummary { scanned: s.scanned, matched: n, top, scan_limit: None, end: s.end }));
    check_complete(s.end, n)
}
```

(`Continuation` and `continuation` are imported from core; `peek` keeps using `continuation(&page)`.) Delete the CLI's `is_special_target`; `check_output` calls `dispatched_core::export::is_special` instead, so one function decides what a special target is:

```rust
pub fn check_output(path: &str, force: bool) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let p = std::path::Path::new(path);
    if path != "-" && !force && p.exists() && !dispatched_core::export::is_special(p) {
        anyhow::bail!(exists_message(path));
    }
    Ok(())
}
```
